Sell-side estimates

❌ D423 questions
C-6740C-6945C-7441

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The sell-side's model is a revenue story it believes and a margin story it has been trimming: revenue to FY2027 is credible and anchored by segment data, FY2028 and beyond depend on the FDA's date and rest on four and then two brokers, and the margin path requires a six-point EBITDA step in FY2027 that the company's own commentary (investment continues; services slow; kidney constrained) argues against, while D&A and tax look too light against the amortisation the company has now disclosed. The company's record is of revenue beats, profit disappointments and late dates, which is exactly the pattern of the last twelve months of revisions (near years cut, far year raised). Estimates have been cut but not capitulated, there is no guidance to rebase, and Q3 will produce a mechanical near-year upgrade that does not resolve the FY2027 question. For the model: revenue near consensus to FY2027 with a lung customer step-down built in, EBITDA margin two to four points below consensus in FY2027 and FY2028, D&A 15 to 30 above the consensus gap, tax 21 to 24 percent, and FY2028 EPS 20 to 30 percent below consensus unless the FDA decides in 2027.

C-67

Sell-side forecasts for the next one to three years against the last one to five; credibility of the gap

❌ D40

Question asked How do the sell-side's forecasts for the next one to three years compare with the last one to five years, for the main financial statement line items in particular and the granular working up to them, and how credible is the gap given the change forces and foundations found above?

Judgement. The sell-side's revenue path is credible to FY2027 and dependent on one regulatory decision from FY2028; its margin path requires a reversal of two trends (gross margin down, EBITDA margin flat) that the record has not yet shown over a full year; and its below-EBITDA lines (D&A, tax, financial items) look too light against what the company has now disclosed. The gap between history and forecast is therefore most credible at the revenue line, least credible at the EPS line, and the Consumer should centre the model below consensus on margin and amortisation and near consensus on revenue through FY2027.

History against forecast, line by line (FactSet history; brokers'-basis actuals and consensus medians).

  • Revenue. FY2021 to FY2025: 258, 415, 598, 822, 812 (+44, +61, +44, +38, -1 percent); consensus 995 / 1,210 / 1,606 / 2,137 (+22 / +22 / +33 / +33 percent). The +22 percent years are below the company's 2022 to 2024 pace and above its 2025; the +33 percent years require United States heart and liver launches on schedule. By segment (FactSet consensus, next three years): thoracic 666 / 822 / 1,054 (lung 572 / 647 / 723; heart 92.5 / 181 / 336); abdominal 269 / 332 / 435 (liver 178 / 212 / 288; kidney 91.5 / 127 / 172); services 62.7 / 75.7 / 87.0. Nine-month 2026 actuals: lung about 440, heart 55, abdominal 193, services 44.6. Reading: FY2026 lung needs about 132 in Q4 (fine); heart needs about 37 in Q4 (a step up from 22, plausible with the CE mark and France but not certain); abdominal needs about 76 in Q4 (a stretch with kidney constrained); services needs about 18 (a stretch). FY2027 lung at +13 percent must absorb the largest customer's decline; heart at 181 matches RedEye's 182 and DNB Carnegie's 194 and assumes reimbursement progress outside France; services at +21 percent assumes a recovery that has not started.
  • Gross margin. FactSet basis 66 to 70 percent in FY2022 to FY2025 (company basis 74 to 75 percent); consensus gross profit 713 / 879 / 1,181 implies 71.7 / 72.6 / 73.5 percent on one broker. Nine-month 2026 is 70 to 71 percent (company basis) and falling on mix. Credible only if heart converts to commercial pricing and abdominal recovers.
  • EBITDA margin (brokers' basis). FY2023 to FY2025: 13.6, 21.8, 19.8 percent; consensus 20.7 / 26.8 / 28.9 / 34.1 percent. The six-point step in FY2027 is the crux: it requires services to reach breakeven (worth about 1.5 points), abdominal to recover five to nine points (about 1.5 to 2.5 points), heart mix and operating leverage (the rest) while management says investment continues. The dispersion across brokers for FY2028 EBITDA (432 to 564) shows how little the sell-side agrees.
  • D&A and EBIT. History D&A 67 to 70 a year; consensus EBITDA-to-EBIT gaps 86 / 110 / 132 / 162. The CE-mark asset alone adds 39 a year from August 2026, the PMA and liver assets will add on approval, and 131.7 was capitalised in nine months 2026; the FY2026 gap of 86 looks light by 10 to 20 and the FY2027 gap of 110 is at the low end of plausible.
  • Tax, interest, shares. Implied consensus tax about 24 / 19 / 19 / 18 percent against a Swedish rate of 20.6 percent and rising United States profits; FY2026 pre-tax (135) above EBIT (120) implies financial income, presumably currency gains, which is not a forecastable line. Shares 31.5 to 31.7 million, plus up to 1.35 percent dilution.
  • EPS. FY2024 to FY2025: 5.74, 1.03; consensus 3.38 / 6.08 / 9.47 / 14.80. The 12-month revision history is -21.5 percent for FY2026, -13.6 percent for FY2027 and +7.1 percent for FY2028; the three-month history is -4.4, -5.7 and -2.7 percent; the shape is cuts in the near years and faith in the far year, which is the "groupthink" pattern the change-forces report warns of. Nine-month reported EPS of 3.08 means FY2026 consensus will likely be revised up after Q3; that will flatter the near-year momentum without changing the FY2027 to FY2028 question.
  • Cash flow and capital expenditure. Consensus operating cash flow 137 / 224 / 328 / 461; capital expenditure 210 / 226 / 240 / 245; free cash flow -99 / 0 / 103 / 216. The capital-expenditure line at 21 to 15 percent of revenue is the sell-side's own acknowledgement that capitalisation continues, which is inconsistent with the light D&A line.
  • Coverage. Four brokers for FY2026 to FY2028, two for FY2029 (and one for net income), so FY2029 is not a consensus in any useful sense.

Credibility of the gap, given the change forces and foundations. The foundations support a revenue line that compounds at about 20 percent (industry demand, lung engine, European heart); they do not support a margin line that rises six points in one year while the company builds a services node, carries a manufacturing transfer, and runs three launches. The honest centre is an EBITDA margin in the low-to-mid 20s for FY2027 and the high 20s for FY2028, with EPS in FY2027 of roughly 4.5 to 5.5 against consensus 6.08, and FY2028 EPS depending on the FDA's date.

C-69

Record against guidance and estimates over one, three and five years, in comparable conditions

❌ D45

Question asked What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

Judgement. Against the sell-side, the record is good at the revenue line and poor at the profit line and at timing; against guidance there is no record because there is none. One year: sales beat consensus in four of the last five quarters (Q4 2025 +4 percent, Q1 2026 +4 percent, Q2 +2 percent, Q3 +6 percent; FY2025 -1 percent), EBITDA beat in two (Q4 2025 +31 percent; Q3 2026 +14 percent) and missed in two (Q1 and Q2 2026, -6 percent each), EPS missed in Q2 (0.52 against 0.70). Three years: 2023 to 2024 were beats on growth (Q2 2024 a 10 percent sales and 44 percent EBITDA beat) with one-off misses on write-downs (Q4 2023 EBITDA; Q4 2024 EBIT -39 percent on write-downs); 2025 was a miss year (Q2 2025 sales -15 percent, a 30 percent one-day fall; the CE delay; EPS consensus for FY2025 fell 35 percent over twelve months to 1.03). Five years: revenue has met or beaten the sell-side's direction in every year but 2025; margins have been lumpier than modelled (EBITDA margin 13 to 25 percent quarter to quarter). In comparable conditions, that is in a growth phase with easy comparatives, the company beats on revenue and the market still cuts the profit numbers (Q1 and Q2 2026). The company's regulatory dates have slipped in every instance before the CE mark and the PMA, which is the timing record the Consumer should weight. Net: expect revenue to land at or above consensus in the near term, expect the profit lines to be revised down between results, and expect dates to be late.

C-74

Extent of estimate cuts across all analysts; guidance rebased lower, kitchen-sinked or sandbagged

❌ D41

Question asked To what extent have sell-side estimates been cut deeply and fully across all analysts, and has company guidance recently been rebased lower, or more aggressively kitchen-sinked or sandbagged?

Judgement. Estimates have been cut meaningfully but not deeply or fully, no guidance has been rebased because none exists, and nothing has been kitchen-sinked or sandbagged; the cutting cycle looks paused rather than complete, and the Q3 beat will now push the near year up.

The cuts: FY2027 consensus EPS -13 percent over twelve months, EBITDA -6 percent, sales -5 percent, target price about flat (+1 percent over six months, -3 percent over three); FY2026 EPS -21.5 percent over twelve months; FY2028 EPS +7.1 percent over twelve months. The dashboard's revision-momentum score is -10 and its sell-side momentum pin is F, the second-worst pattern in the peer set, but that reflects the direction of near-year cuts, not their depth. Breadth: only two of the four to five brokers published after Q2 (both cut), DNB Carnegie's cuts after Q2 were 6 to 7 percent on EPS, and target prices span 280 (Pareto) to 353 (Stifel) with 100 percent Buy ratings, which is not capitulation. The reasons for the cuts were margins and operating expenses (gross margin assumptions, launch costs, a 12 percent services cut at RedEye), with currency secondary; revenue lines were held. Guidance: the 2027 targets were reaffirmed in July and not restated at Q3; there is no sandbagging because there is no number to sandbag, and the one kitchen-sink opportunity (writing down the services unit or the FlowHawk acquisition) has not been taken. For the model: the estimate base is partly cut, not trough-level; the FY2026 figure will rise after Q3; the FY2027 to FY2028 figures remain exposed to a margin and timing reset, and the sell-side's unanimity means the next move on a slip is down.

Stock market narrative

C581 question
C-2260

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The stock market narrative has a long and dated staircase to climb and has already climbed its first two steps (the CE mark; the PMA receipt), so the market's attention now sits on three things: the speed of the European heart ramp from Q4 2026, the FDA's handling of the heart PMA through 2027, and whether recovered lung demand can replace a customer that has turned supplier while services returns to breakeven. The staircase is good enough to carry a positive narrative through 2027 if the quarterly European heart and lung numbers cooperate, and the two most novel steps (the liver 510(k); a CMS coverage route) are under-modelled upside; but nothing on it is a surprise to the four brokers, the sector rewards the first beat-and-raise rather than a beat alone and this company does not guide, and the largest step is a regulator's date that has already slipped once in this programme's history. For the model: date the steps on the regulators' timetable plus a lag, count the European heart ramp as the narrative's load-bearing leg through 2027, and treat the United States heart decision as the single event that sets whether FY2028 looks like consensus or a year behind it.

C-22

The staircase of novel positive developments: 0 to 6 months, 6 to 18, 18 to 36; novelty; foundation

C60

Question asked What staircase of novel positive developments lies ahead: which new, dated things can the market look forward to within the next 6 months, in months 6 to 18, and in months 18 to 36; how novel is each; and how well-founded?

Judgement. The staircase is long, mostly dated and mostly company-delivered in substance but regulator-timed in date; almost none of it is novel to the four brokers or to the company's own communication, so the steps move the shares by resolving binaries rather than by surprising anyone. Two of the near steps (the heart CE mark; the PMA receipt) have already been climbed in the last three months, which is why the narrative has moved from "will the regulator approve in Europe" to "how fast does Europe adopt and when does the FDA decide".

Within six months (to April 2027).

  • The Q4 2026 report in late January 2027: the first quarter of commercial European heart sales (consensus implies about 37 in Q4 against 22 in Q3), the first quarter after the Q3 timing effects, the first read on the largest customer's purchasing, and the first quarter with the CE-mark amortisation. Well-founded and dated; novelty low.
  • Completion of the tenfold disposable-capacity project and the abdominal manufacturing consolidation in Q4 2026, with unit-cost benefits promised from 2027. Dated; novelty low; a validation failure is the risk.
  • A permanent CFO appointment (search since February 2026). Undated; plausible; novel only as a relief.
  • Fourth and fifth OPO hubs (three activated; target four to five), and the OPO decertification transition from late 2026 with agreements expiring in January 2027, which disrupts and then accelerates. Partly dated.
  • The FDA Q-sub outcome on the Liver Assist 510(k) route (expected late 2026) and an FDA advisory-panel date for the heart PMA, if any. Both undated; the liver one is the most novel step in the near term because RedEye has not capitalised it.
  • Kidney growth resuming in early 2027 after the component replacement. Dated by the company.
  • The Riksbank's November 2026 decision (a hike is likely) and its effect on the krona and on Swedish small caps: a headwind step, not a positive one.
  • A short squeeze on any of the above, with 8 to 9.5 percent of shares short.

Months 6 to 18 (April 2027 to April 2028).

  • The European heart ramp through 2027 across the approximately 30 priority centres (RedEye 182; DNB Carnegie 194 for 2027), with reimbursement decisions in the Netherlands, Belgium, Austria and the United Kingdom as the gating items. Well-founded; the key test of the European leg.
  • The Liver Assist 510(k) submission in H1 2027. Dated by the company; novel in that it could pull a 2029 launch to early 2028.
  • The broader United States kidney launch in 2027. Dated.
  • The FDA decision on the heart PMA, late 2027 to early 2028 on RedEye's reading; United States commercial launch "targeted" for end-2027 subject to approval; CMS coverage via the RAPID pathway as the follow-on. The single largest step on the staircase; dated only approximately; the one the price pays for.
  • The FY2027 report (January 2028) as the test of the 2027 margin targets. Dated; the market will judge the company's credibility on it.
  • Lung Bioengineering's step-down becoming visible in the lung line, which is a negative step the market will net against the positives.

Months 18 to 36 (April 2028 to October 2029).

  • The United States heart commercial ramp and consumables pull-through (consensus heart 336 in FY2028, most of the growth from the United States). Well-founded if approved; the size is the open question.
  • The United States liver launch in early 2028 if the 510(k) is accepted, otherwise a resumed trial. Conditional.
  • The end of the regulatory capitalisation cycle and the turn in free cash flow (consensus 103 in FY2028, 216 in FY2029). Dated by arithmetic.
  • The revolver's maturity in January 2028, which is a neutral step the market will watch for refinancing or a raise.
  • Longer-dated and least founded: NRP and FlowHawk becoming meaningful; a strategic approach from a consolidator (Terumo and Getinge have paid 11 to 20x sales for peers), which is not a step to model but is a floor.

Novelty and foundation, summarised. The near steps are well-founded and known; the mid steps are well-founded in the European leg and binary in the United States leg; the far steps are conditional on the mid steps. The two developments with any claim to novelty, the liver 510(k) acceleration and a CMS RAPID coverage route, are the ones the sell-side has not modelled, and they are also the two with the least evidence on file. The staircase supports a narrative that improves each quarter through 2027 if the European heart numbers arrive and worsens sharply on any FDA slip, which is the same binary shape the price has carried for two years.
