Financial items No hedging policy; 55 percent of sales in dollars, 35 percent in euros; management's sensitivity: 10 percent on the dollar is about 45 of sales and two margin points Pre-tax profit Low: the revaluation of 250 to 300 of foreign-currency cash is unforecastable (+32.7 in 2024; -66.6 in 2025; about +34 in nine months 2026) A currency view
Tax No guidance; 34.7 percent (2025), 15 percent (nine months 2026), a Q3 credit; deferred tax assets 21 at end-2025 with United States losses unrecognised Net income Low A rate (ours 16, 20, 21, 22, 23 percent) and a view on United States recognition
Shares Incentive plans dilute at most 1.35 percent; a 10 percent issuance authority, unused EPS High Nothing

Reading down the table, the clarity is high for four links, medium for four and low for five, and the low ones are all below the gross-profit line. The two brokers' models show the consequence: DNB Carnegie's 2027 sales of 1,276 and EBITDA 319 become net income of 180 (D&A 105, tax 15 percent); RedEye's 1,162 and 272 become 99 (D&A about 117, tax 21 percent, and a financial-items assumption I cannot see). Our 1,162 and 279 become 121. A 10 percent spread at the revenue line is a 45 percent spread at the EBITDA line and an 80 percent spread at net income, and the widening is entirely in the links the company leaves to the reader.

The external chain. Four value-chain dynamics transmit to the lines as follows. (1) DCD donors and the OPO rule (upheld 28 September 2026; agreements expire January 2027) transmit to United States EVLP volumes with a one-to-two-quarter disruption first, then acceleration; the direction is clear and corroborated by competitor growth of 37 to 65 percent, the company's own volumes are not disclosed, and the own-4 report notes an expert view that the rule's direct effect on volumes has so far been "minimal". Clarity: high in direction, low in magnitude. (2) TransMedics' pricing (USD 60,000 to 110,000 a case; growing surgeon pushback) sets the opening for Xvivo's United States heart price (about half) and the ceiling on its European heart price; transmission to the heart line is clear, to margin it is favourable (thoracic-grade on a low product cost). (3) Bridge to Life (VitaSmart at USD 15,000 to 20,000; NICE funding; USD 110 million raised) and OrganOx (Terumo's target to triple revenue by fiscal 2027) transmit to abdominal price and share; the own-5 report quantifies the European price points (hypothermic cases at EUR 2,000 to 6,000 against EUR 30,000 to 40,000 normothermic), which is why the abdominal margin recovery must come from unit cost. Clarity: high. (4) The krona transmits to every line above and below EBITDA; the company's sensitivity is disclosed, the exposure is not hedged, and the path is the macro forecaster's. Clarity: high on mechanism, nil on outcome. A fifth external actor, the FDA, transmits to 2028 revenue and to the start date of the largest new amortisation line, and the company can say nothing useful about its calendar.

What this means for the model and the memo. The Consumer should present the case as two theses with different evidential standing: an operating thesis (segment revenue and gross profit) that is traceable, disclosed and testable each quarter, and an earnings thesis (EPS) that rests on four assumptions the company does not disclose and the sell-side has not aligned. The memo's forecast tables should carry the EBITDA-to-EPS bridge explicitly for that reason, and the memo's riskiness topic should say that about half of the FY2027 EPS cone is below the gross-profit line.

D-07

Co-movement of the share price and the multiple with sector and industry peers, and so how far the multiple is company-driven

❌ D42

Question asked To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically (on a rolling six-month basis), and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

Judgement. The share price is overwhelmingly company-driven and has become more so: over the latest six-month window its daily moves have a correlation of 0.30 with the equal-weighted basket of dashboard peers, a beta of 0.84 and an R-squared of 9 percent, so 91 percent of its variance is its own; it fell 26.4 percent in a window in which the basket rose 15.7 percent, a 42-point gap that no sector factor explains. Over 24 months the share explained by the basket ranged from 5 percent (February 2025) to a peak of 33 percent (April 2026, during the sector-wide medtech de-rating and the run-up to the CE decision) and has since fallen every month to 9 percent; the beta ran from 0.66 to 1.62 and is now below one. The multiple tells the same story from the other side: Xvivo's P/E sits at the 1st percentile of its own ten-year range while fourteen of seventeen rated peers sit in the bottom decile of theirs, so the level of the multiple has a sector component (the whole consumables cohort has de-rated), but the change in Xvivo's multiple over six months, from about 32 times 24-month-forward consensus EPS (the two-year median) to 27 times while the basket rose, is company news: the Q2 margin miss, the CE-delay memory, the largest customer's platform approval and the Q3 timing items. So the multiple is company-driven at the margin and sector-driven in its floor, and the first set's reading ("the record attributes about 15 percent of volatility to sector compression") is confirmed and if anything overstated for the latest window.

What the co-movement table shows, read carefully. The pairwise correlations in the latest window are all below 0.25 and several are near zero (Alcon 0.02; Straumann 0.03; Smith & Nephew -0.01); the highest are with the smaller growth names (Demant 0.25; Ypsomed 0.22; Sonova 0.21; Bonesupport 0.19; Dynavox, Medacta and Vitrolife 0.20), which is the pattern of a stock that trades with Nordic small-cap growth sentiment on quiet days and on its own news on loud ones. The 24-month path is informative about regime: the R-squared rose steadily from 13 percent (late 2025) to 33 percent (April 2026) as the sector de-rated together under the discount-rate shock and United States medtech's 25 percent fall, and then collapsed to 9 percent as Xvivo's own calendar took over (CE mark 11 August; PMA receipt 28 September; the Lung Bioengineering approval and share-price fall in October; the Q3 report on 22 October). The beta of 1.62 at October 2025 and 0.84 now says the stock amplified sector moves when the sector was the story and ignores them when it is not.

Why this matters for predictability. Three consequences, two negative and one positive. First, a stock that moves on its own news is a stock whose price path is governed by the binary calendar described in D-04 (a customer's transition; a regulator's letters; reimbursement decisions), so the share price is less forecastable than a sector-beta stock's even if the business is more forecastable than peers'. Second, a sector recovery would help little: with a beta of 0.84 and an R-squared of 9 percent, a 20 percent rise in the basket is worth perhaps 5 to 8 percent to Xvivo in expectation, so the "European medtech rebounds through multiple expansion at range lows" pattern the first set cited (C-84) applies to this stock only through its own worries clearing, which is a company matter. Third, and positive, the idiosyncrasy means the re-rating is in the company's hands: the two events that would move the multiple most (European heart quarters that match or beat the sell-side; an FDA decision inside the brokers' window) are company-dated, and the short interest of 8 to 9.5 percent (against under 3 percent for Swedish medtech peers) gives any such event a discontinuous payoff.

Company-driven or industry-driven, for the fair-multiple topic. The guideline fair multiple is set from the stock's phase and its own historical range; this answer says the stock's history is the right reference and the sector's current percentile is not a reason to expect convergence. The stock's five-year 24-month-forward P/E range is 24.8 to 243 times (median 44.8; 25th to 75th percentile 33.8 to 56.5); on consensus EPS the shares are at 27 times (6th percentile), on our EPS about 40 times (roughly the 40th percentile). The fair multiple therefore depends almost entirely on which earnings the market comes to believe, which is a company-driven question, and the Consumer should expect the multiple to move with the FY2027 revision cycle rather than with the sector.

The industry read-across that does bite. One peer is outside the dashboard basket and matters more than any in it: TransMedics. The first set recorded the sympathy moves (Xvivo's multiple contracted when a short-seller report took TransMedics down 49 percent in early 2025; TransMedics fell over 25 percent on its May 2026 miss) and its Q3 2026 results on or about 4 November 2026 are the next such day. That is an industry transmission the co-movement table cannot see because TransMedics is not in the basket, and it is the one exception to "company-driven": perfusion-specific news from the United States bellwether moves Xvivo's multiple even when its own numbers are unchanged.

Back to contents

Layer 4: Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

The fourth set as a whole ❌ D33 written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Our assessment of the foundations and the forecasts is reflected in current guidance, sell-side estimates and the market narrative in direction and not in level, and the level is where the money is. The company's 2027 targets will be missed by a wide margin and re-dated, most probably at or soon after the Q4 2026 report on 28 January 2027; that is already disbelieved by the sell-side (23 to 25 percent EBITDA margins modelled against a target above 30) but not by investors, so it is a narrative event more than an estimate event. The sell-side's FY2027 earnings per share, about 5.8 on the post-Q3 mean and heading toward about 5.5 when all four brokers update, is about 50 percent above our 3.8, and the gap closes through a sequence of cuts at each of the next four reports, driven first by the alignment of amortisation and tax (worth 8 to 10 percent on its own) and then by the largest customer's step-down, the operating-cost build and the krona; the FY2028 consensus, rising toward about 10.5 as RedEye shifts United States heart revenue into 2028, is 40 to 60 percent above our 6.1 on a date the FDA will set in the second half of 2027 or later, with perhaps a one-in-five chance that it sets it the brokers' way. The narrative articulates every dynamic we have identified and quantifies about half; the half it does not quantify is the half below EBITDA, which is why the market's framework (margin trajectory, approvals, a strategic-floor multiple) will be surprised by a flat EPS year it has not modelled, and why the dashboard's valuation rating of A, built on consensus earnings, is incongruent with our own forecasts: on our earnings the stock is at about 40 times 24-month-forward EPS, the middle of its five-year range, not its floor. Two gaps run in the case's favour and should be stated as such in the memo: the business is self-funding (our free cash flow is about -8 and +15 for 2026 and 2027 against the two brokers' -97 and -80, and the balance sheet absorbs a one-year FDA delay without a raise), and our FDA date is at the conservative end of its distribution, so the largest single estimate risk is one we have already taken. For Richard: the market has priced the known worries into the multiple and not into the numbers; the next six to twelve months are a revision cycle the stock has not completed; the case for owning the shares through that cycle rests on the strategic floor, the cash and the European heart quarters, not on the earnings or the guidance, and the case for waiting rests on the probability, which I put at about 70 percent, that the Q4 2026 and Q1 2027 reports deliver cuts to FY2027 consensus before they deliver the European heart evidence that would re-rate the stock.

Guidance

1 question
I-0132
I-01

Likelihood that company guidance is raised, met or reduced at the earnings events over the next 18 months, and what would drive each; secondarily, the mid-term targets for 18 months to three years out

❌ D32

Question asked How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all such events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

Judgement. There is no numerical guidance to raise, meet or reduce, so the question resolves into the company's three kinds of forward statement: its 2027 financial targets (an EBITDA margin above 30 percent, an EBIT margin above 20 percent, a 70 percent abdominal gross margin; restated in the Q3 report without new numbers), its dated operational milestones (United States heart launch "targeted" for end-2027 subject to FDA review; a liver 510(k) submission in H1 2027 for an early-2028 launch; kidney growth resuming in early 2027; four to five hubs; tenfold capacity by Q4 2026), and its one quantitative steer (July 2026: FY2026 EBITDA in line with consensus). My probabilities over the six reports in the window (Q4 2026 on 28 January 2027; Q1 to Q4 2027; Q1 2028): the 2027 financial targets will not be met (probability above 90 percent on our 24 percent EBITDA and 13.6 percent EBIT margins; even DNB Carnegie's post-Q3 25.0 percent and RedEye's 23.4 percent miss them) and will be re-dated, reframed or quietly dropped, with about a 55 percent probability of that happening at the Q4 2026 report or the FY2026 annual report in March 2027, about 85 percent by the Q2 2027 report in July, and the residual being the company carrying a visibly missed target into the FY2027 report in January 2028, which would be a credibility event. The United States heart launch date will be moved into 2028 in company language during 2027 (probability about 75 percent; the brokers have already moved it) and the first Q4 2027 or Q1 2028 report is the likely moment. The FY2026 EBITDA steer will be met approximately (our 199 against the post-Q3 brokers' 199 to 200 and the pre-results 206; the Q3 beat was one-third timing and Q4 carries hiring). The milestones most likely to be met or exceeded are the hub count (a fourth and fifth hub are in a pipeline of five), the liver 510(k) submission (dialogue with the FDA "progressing well", on the company's account) and the kidney relaunch; the one most likely to slip beyond its date is the abdominal margin target. Nothing in the record (no numerical guidance ever; targets reaffirmed through setbacks; warnings against extrapolating strong quarters) suggests the company will raise anything; the only "raise" available to it is a new, later set of targets at a capital-markets day, which would be read as a reset rather than a raise. Secondarily, any new target for 2028 to 2029 is more likely to be set below the current 2027 numbers (an EBITDA margin "approaching 30 percent" by 2028 or 2029, in line with our 27 to 29.5 percent and with RedEye's 29 percent for 2028) than above them.

What would drive each outcome, report by report.

  • Q4 2026 (28 January 2027). The report that most likely carries the re-dating. Drivers of a reduction: the full-year EBITDA margin landing at 20 percent against a 30 percent target twelve months out; the first full quarter of CE-mark amortisation (9.75) and a Q4 EBIT margin in the low teens making the 20 percent EBIT target arithmetically remote; RedEye's post-Q3 expectation that management "will likely be forced to re-date or lower its targets during the upcoming fourth-quarter report"; and the FY2026 annual report (March 2027), which must restate the strategy period. Drivers of a hold: management's habit of reaffirming (July 2026, after a 6 percent EBITDA miss) and the absence of any requirement to guide. Drivers of an effective raise: a Q4 heart figure above 30 and two Tier 2 reimbursement decisions would let management keep the targets as "ambition" with a straight face. What the company says about the 2027 targets at this report is the single most useful guidance datum in the window: a re-dating to 2028 to 2029 would align the company with our model and remove a credibility overhang; a reaffirmation would set up a larger disappointment in January 2028.
  • Q1 2027 (April). The first quarter with the largest customer on its own platform (United Therapeutics says LungFX joins its commercial services in 2027), the first OPO-transition quarter (agreements expired January 2027) and the first commercial heart quarter. Guidance-relevant drivers: whether management quantifies the customer's run-rate (it has declined to so far); whether kidney growth has resumed; the first sight of the Swedish plant's cost; the CFO appointment. A Q1 in which lung excluding timing is below 130 or services is still below -10 percent gross margin would make any reaffirmation of the 2027 targets untenable.
  • Q2 2027 (July). The anniversary of the 2026 reaffirmation. The liver 510(k) should have been filed (H1 2027); the heart PMA will be about nine months into review, past the Day-100 meeting and into the window in which a major deficiency letter (92 percent of the 2026 cohort) or a panel listing would have appeared. Drivers: the FDA's posture; the European heart quarterly figure against our 33 and RedEye's path to 200-plus for the year. This is my modal date for a formal re-dating if it has not happened in January.
  • Q3 2027 (October) and Q4 2027 (January 2028). The reports on which the United States launch date is tested against its "end-2027" target. Our base has approval in late 2028; the brokers have Q4 2027 to Q1 2028 (RedEye) and mid-2027 (DNB Carnegie's base, with late 2027 to early 2028 as its risk case). Drivers of a reduction: no approval by Q4 2027 (probability about 85 percent on the own-3 report's base rates); drivers of a hold: a panel date or an approvable letter that lets management keep "launch in 2028". The FY2027 report (January 2028) is also where the 2027 targets are either met (they will not be) or formally retired.
  • Q1 2028 (April 2028). First read on the United States launch organisation's cost against revenue if approval has arrived; otherwise a quarter of continued capitalisation and opex ahead of a decision.

Why the company's guidance behaviour matters more than usual here. The first set established (C-70, C-71) that the company has never guided numerically and reaffirms targets through setbacks. The I1 report adds the sell-side's reading: RedEye expects the targets to be re-dated and treats 2028 as "the more relevant investment horizon"; DNB Carnegie keeps the targets as long-term milestones while modelling 25 percent. So the sell-side has already discounted the targets, which is why the first consequence of a re-dating would be modest for estimates (the brokers are at 23 to 25 percent) and larger for the narrative (the company would be conceding, for the first time, that a stated target has slipped). The second consequence is the one the Consumer should weigh: a company that re-dates once at a credibility low has an incentive to set the new target where it can beat it, and the sector rewards the first beat-and-raise (the first set's C-84; Bonesupport's 2026 cycle). A re-dating to "above 30 percent by 2029" would therefore be a negative headline and a positive set-up; a reaffirmation would be a neutral headline and a negative set-up.

Peers' guidance behaviour as a check. Ambu nudged its organic growth guidance down to about 10 percent in August 2026 while holding its margin path; Getinge beat on Q3 2026 margins (adjusted EBITA +18 percent) and holds its targets; Bonesupport is in a beat-and-raise cycle on a payment decision; Vitrolife missed its 10 percent organic growth target and restructured. Xvivo's position in that set is "targets set before three adverse developments (services losses, the customer's platform approval, the CE-mark amortisation) and not yet reset", which is the Vitrolife pattern in guidance terms, redeemed by the Bonesupport pattern in clinical terms.

The gap, for scoring. Our assessment sits far below the company's 2027 targets (24 against above 30 on EBITDA; 13.6 against above 20 on EBIT; 63 against 70 on abdominal gross margin) and a year behind its United States launch target. The gap is unfavourable: the direction of guidance change over the next 18 months is down or sideways, never up, and the one favourable reading (a credible reset that the company then beats) is conditional on management behaviour it has not shown before. The partial mitigant is that the sell-side already disbelieves the targets, so the estimate consequence of a re-dating is small; the narrative consequence is not.

Sell-side estimates

1 question
I-0228
I-02

Likelihood that sell-side consensus forecasts and the net breadth of revisions move higher or lower over the next few earnings events, and why; secondarily, consensus for 18 months to three years out

❌ D28

Question asked How likely are sell-side consensus forecasts, and the net breadth of revisions, to move higher or lower over the next few earnings events (over the next 18 months): to be upgraded, cut or held, and why? Secondarily, how likely are the consensus forecasts for 18 months to three years out to be upgraded, cut or held?

Judgement. The consensus will move in three directions at once over the next six months and then in one direction for the following twelve. In the next six months: FY2026 EPS rises mechanically (from the pre-results 3.38 toward about 3.8; DNB Carnegie is already at 3.90 on tax and financial items, RedEye at about 3.6, and our corrected figure is about 3.75), FY2027 EPS falls (DNB Carnegie cut 4 percent to 5.70; RedEye's post-Q3 net income of 99 is about 3.1 a share; the FactSet mean was already 5.81 on 16 October against a 6.08 median; the full consensus once Pareto and Stifel update should settle near 5.5), and FY2028 EPS rises or holds (RedEye raised 2028 revenue 8 percent to 1,720 and models heart revenue above 550 on a 2028 United States launch; DNB Carnegie cut 3 percent to 8.60; the median of the four, about 10.5, would be above the pre-results 9.47). The pattern is the same "near year up, next year down, far year up" shape the first set identified from the twelve-month revision history, and it means the sell-side is pushing the heart ramp out rather than down: the sum of its 2027 and 2028 heart revenue is roughly preserved while its 2027 EPS is cut. From the Q4 2026 report onward I expect FY2027 consensus EPS to be cut at every one of the next four reports, by a cumulative 25 to 35 percent from the pre-results 6.08 toward 4.0 to 4.5 by the Q3 2027 report, driven in order by the alignment of amortisation (worth 8 to 10 percent on the I1 report's calculation), the first disclosed quarters of the largest customer's step-down, the operating-cost run-rate, the tax rate's normalisation and the krona; FY2027 revenue consensus to be cut 3 to 5 percent (1,210 toward 1,160) and EBITDA 10 to 15 percent (324 toward 280 to 290). FY2028 consensus EPS (9.47, rising to about 10.5) is unlikely to be cut much before the FDA's posture is visible, which on the own-3 report's base rates is mid-to-late 2027; then, on our dating, it falls 30 to 40 percent in one or two steps as the United States launch moves from 2028 into 2029 in the brokers' models. The net breadth of revisions stays negative throughout (three of four brokers have cut FY2027 since July; the dashboard's revision-momentum score is -10 and its sell-side momentum pin is F) and the 100 percent Buy share is at risk from one downgrade (Pareto, whose 280 target is the lowest and nearest the price). Against our model: the FY2027 consensus is heading toward our 3.8 but is unlikely to reach it inside 18 months, so each report will carry a cut and the stock will trade through a revision cycle it has not yet completed; the FY2028 consensus will stay 40 to 60 percent above our 6.1 until the FDA timing is known, which is the largest unresolved gap in the case.

Where consensus sits against the run-rate and against us, line by line.

  • FY2026. Revenue 995 (we 993; nine months 733; Q4 needs 262 against 226 a year earlier, which our 260 at +15 percent nearly meets); EBITDA 206 pre-results (we 199; DNB Carnegie 200; RedEye 199; the compilation-defined "miss" of 3 percent is the Q3 timing items not recurring); EPS 3.38 pre-results (we about 3.75 after my corrections; DNB Carnegie 3.90; nine-month reported 3.08 plus a Q4 of about 0.6 adjusted). Direction: up on EPS, flat on revenue, slightly down on EBITDA. The Q4 report will therefore be a "beat" on EPS and "in line" on sales and EBITDA against a compilation that will already have risen; the market will not pay for an EPS beat made of tax and currency, and the I1 report's warning that the near-year upgrade "masks" the outer-year cuts is right.
  • FY2027. Revenue 1,210 pre-results (DNB Carnegie 1,276; RedEye 1,162; Pareto 1,188; Stifel 1,334; we 1,162). EBITDA 324 (DNB Carnegie 319; RedEye 272; Pareto 300; Stifel 372; we 279). EPS 6.08 median, 5.81 mean (DNB Carnegie 5.70; RedEye about 3.1; Pareto 6.76 and Stifel 6.71, both stale; we 3.8). The post-Q3 spread between the two updated brokers (3.1 to 5.7 on EPS; 272 to 319 on EBITDA) is the widest in the stock's coverage history and is itself the forecast: when two informed analysts differ by 80 percent on net income for the next calendar year, the consensus is not a view but an average of views, and it moves toward whichever the quarters support. Our model says the quarters will support the lower.
  • FY2028. Revenue 1,606 (range 1,501 to 1,726; we 1,476); EBITDA 464 (we 398); EPS 9.47 (DNB Carnegie 8.60; RedEye 11.8; Pareto 10.05; Stifel 11.23; we 6.1). The brokers' FY2028 rests on a United States heart launch in late 2027 or early 2028 and on United States liver from early 2028; ours on a United States heart launch in Q1 2029. The gap is a date, not a view of the product, and it will close on the FDA's calendar rather than on company results.
  • FY2029 (two brokers). Revenue 2,137, EBITDA 730, EPS 14.80 against our 1,845, 544 and 8.75. Not a consensus in any useful sense; it will fall mechanically when the third and fourth brokers extend their models, because the two that have done so are the more optimistic on the United States.

What would be needed for FY2027 consensus to be held, raised or cut at each of the next four reports.

  1. Q4 2026 (28 January 2027). Held if: Q4 heart at or above the compilation's implied figure (about 37 pre-results; the post-Q3 compilation will be lower, perhaps 30), lung excluding timing above 140, a Q4 EBITDA margin near 20 percent and the 2027 targets reaffirmed. Cut if: heart at 25 or below (the derogation run-rate with December's first CE-marked invoices), a disclosed step in the customer's ordering, a first full quarter of CE-mark amortisation that forces the two unaligned brokers to add 25 to 30 to their 2027 D&A, a Q4 tax charge near 20 percent that ends the "15 percent" assumption, and a re-dating of the targets. My probability of a net cut to FY2027 EPS at this report: about 70 percent, mostly from the D&A and tax alignment rather than from operations. Raised: only if heart exceeds 35 and management quantifies the customer's step-down at the low end (about 40), probability about 10 percent.
  2. Q1 2027 (April). Cut if lung excluding timing falls below 130 (the customer transitioning faster than 65 a year), services is still below -10 percent, opex is above 140, or kidney has not resumed; held if heart is above 32 and two Tier 2 reimbursements are announced. Probability of a cut: about 65 percent; this is the report where revenue, not just the below-EBITDA lines, is first tested.
  3. Q2 2027 (July). Cut if the abdominal gross margin after the transfer is below 63 percent, if H1 EBITDA margin is below 22 percent, or if the FDA has issued a major deficiency letter; held or raised if heart exceeds 40 in the quarter and the liver 510(k) has been filed with no clinical-study requirement. Probability of a cut: about 55 percent.
  4. Q3 2027 (October). The FDA report: a cut to FY2028 (not FY2027) if no approval or panel date is in sight; a raise to FY2028 if approval has come. FY2027 consensus should by then be within 10 to 15 percent of ours, and the Q3 2027 comparison against a Q3 2026 inflated by 19 of timing items is the last mechanical headwind.

Below-EBITDA alignment, which is the quiet driver. The I1 report's test is the right one: DNB Carnegie's 2027 model carries development amortisation of 38 (the CE-mark asset alone is 39, before the roughly 25 a year of other completed projects), a paid tax rate of 15 percent through 2028, and net financial items near zero; RedEye carries 21 percent tax and heavier D&A (its EBITDA-to-EBIT gap of 117 is close to our 131 total). Aligning the whole consensus to a 39-plus-25 amortisation line and a 20 percent tax rate takes 8 to 10 percent off FY2027 EPS before a single operating number changes. That alignment will happen piecemeal (the Q4 report shows 9.75 of CE-mark amortisation in one quarter; the FY2026 annual report shows the tax note), which is why I expect cuts at every report rather than one reset.

Peers' latest results as read-across for the direction of estimates. Getinge's Q3 2026 (21 October) beat on margin with consumables up 6 percent and Paragonix "outstanding", which supports the demand side of the thesis and nothing on Xvivo's margin. Terumo's Organ Technologies grew 50 percent year on year in its latest reported quarter with a 20 percent margin target, and Terumo raised guidance in August 2026: liver perfusion demand is strong, pricing is competitive, and the abdominal line's revenue consensus is safer than its margin consensus. Bonesupport's Q3 2026 (39 percent growth; 34 percent adjusted EBIT margin) is the sector's live beat-and-raise and will make the contrast with Xvivo's cutting cycle more visible to the same Nordic investor base. TransMedics' Q3 2026 on or about 4 November is the next external datum: its heart and liver volumes and any comment on pricing pressure read directly into the brokers' United States ramp assumptions for Xvivo, and its last two results days moved its own shares by 25 percent or more.

Secondarily, 18 months to three years out. The FY2028 and FY2029 consensus will be cut, in my judgement, by 30 to 40 percent on EPS over the next 18 months, but late in the window and in one or two steps keyed to the FDA, with a 15 to 20 percent chance that an approval inside the brokers' window leaves them intact or raised. The direction of travel at RedEye (2028 revenue up 8 percent on the shift of heart revenue into 2028) shows that the sell-side's response to a slower 2027 is to load 2028, which raises the stakes on the one date.

The gap, for scoring. Our FY2027 EPS is about 35 percent below the post-Q3 consensus and our FY2028 about 40 percent below; our revenue is 4 and 8 percent below; our EBITDA 14 percent below in both years. The gap is unfavourable in the sense that matters for the next six to twelve months: consensus has to come toward us through a sequence of cuts, the breadth of revisions is already negative and the rating share has only one way to move. Two features make it less unfavourable than the arithmetic suggests: part of the gap (D&A, tax, currency) is non-operating and some holders will look through it to cash, where our view is above the two brokers who model it (DNB Carnegie's free cash flow of -97 and -80 for 2026 and 2027 against our -8 and +15); and the FY2028 gap is a date whose resolution could, with perhaps a one-in-five chance, go the brokers' way.

Stock market narrative

1 question
I-0338
I-03

How comprehensively the dynamics are articulated in sell-side and investor commentary, and how well known each is

❌ D38

Question asked How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in the current narrative of (i) sell-side commentary and (ii) investor commentary (for example on Twitter): how well known is each of them to investors who own or cover the company or its sector, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to three years out?

Judgement. The sell-side narrative, which in practice is two houses (DNB Carnegie and RedEye publish on every event and were the only two the I2 report could find documents for; Pareto and Stifel appear in FactSet but not in the research record), articulates every one of the fifteen dynamics the second set identified in words, articulates about half of them in numbers, and prices the below-EBITDA half of them wrongly or not at all; the investor narrative (Swedish retail forums, the RedEye community, Twitter) articulates three of the fifteen (the heart approval and ramp; the FDA timeline; the largest customer's defection) and none of the financial-bridge items, and reads the Q3 net-profit beat as operational. The narrative is therefore an EBITDA-margin-and-approval story in which the risks are well known in direction and badly known in size, and the dynamics that would most surprise the market when the Q4 2026 and FY2027 numbers arrive are, in order: the level of FY2027 earnings per share (the compounding of a modest EBITDA shortfall through amortisation, tax and currency into a flat EPS year); the size of the largest customer's step-down, which both brokers anchor to DNB Carnegie's 5 to 6 percent of 2027 revenue without contract evidence; the extent to which the 2028 consensus now depends on a 2028 United States launch (RedEye's heart revenue above 550 in 2028); and, favourably, the cash generation, which neither broker models as positive before 2028. One further point is known to nobody in the narrative and is the most important for the memo: the "cheap on earnings" framing (a P/E at the 1st percentile of ten years; 27 times 24-month-forward consensus) is a function of consensus EPS, and on our EPS the multiple is about 40 times and mid-range, so the valuation narrative and the earnings narrative cannot both be right.

The fifteen dynamics, who articulates each, and how well it is known.

  1. The largest customer's step-down (Lung Bioengineering; LungFX approved June 2026; purchases to fall from 2027; fluids retained). Widely known in direction: both brokers since October 2026, DNB Carnegie's 5 to 6 percent sizing (7 and 9 October) adopted by RedEye (23 October); retail forums reacted negatively and drove the October share-price fall. Not known in size or timing: the own-1 report finds no contract terms, no fluid volumes per run, no pricing; the 10 inventory build in Q3 is noted by both brokers as a Q4 headwind. Verdict: the direction is fully priced, the range (40 to 100) is not; a fast transition would surprise.
  2. Amortisation of regulatory assets (39 a year CE-mark; 800.7 to 919.9 of capitalised development). RedEye states the 39 explicitly and treats it as non-cash; DNB Carnegie models development amortisation of 38 for 2027, which cannot contain it; retail ignores it entirely. Verdict: known to one broker, mis-modelled by the other, unknown to investors; a surprise in the FY2027 EBIT line of perhaps 25 to 30 against the lagging models.
  3. Tax (34.7 percent in 2025; 15 percent for nine months 2026 with a Q3 credit; United States losses unrecognised). DNB Carnegie models 15 percent through 2028; RedEye 21 percent; retail celebrates the net-profit beat. Verdict: known as a line, not as a normalising rate; the Q4 charge and the FY2026 tax note will surprise the lower assumption.
  4. Unhedged currency (Q3 gains of 7.1; about +34 of nine-month financial items). Both brokers note the exposure and DNB Carnegie attributes part of its FY2026 upgrade to it; retail misreads it as operating. Verdict: known as a risk, not carried as a 2027 drag.
  5. Services at -19 percent gross margin. Both brokers discuss it every quarter; RedEye reads it as launch infrastructure, DNB Carnegie as a drag; the former director's account (surgeons lost; late to NRP; "poor management") is in expert calls only. Verdict: well known; the disagreement is about meaning, not fact; a continued negative margin in Q1 2027 would move the DNB reading to consensus.
  6. The Q3 timing items (8.5 of lease recognition; 10 of inventory). Both brokers adjusted (RedEye: underlying growth 29 percent, still a 5 percent beat); retail did not. Verdict: known to professionals, not to the public; the Q4 comparison will reveal it.
  7. The 2027 margin targets. RedEye says the company will fall short (23 percent EBITDA) and treats 2028 as the relevant year; DNB Carnegie models 25 percent while describing the targets as realistic milestones; retail treats them as guidance. Verdict: known to be stale by the sell-side, not by investors; a re-dating would surprise retail more than brokers.
  8. The European heart ramp (RedEye 87 to 182 to 351 for 2026 to 2028; DNB Carnegie about 180 for 2027). The most articulated dynamic in the whole narrative; both brokers, every note since August; retail's main topic. Tier 2 reimbursement timing is named (France done; the Netherlands, Austria, Belgium, the United Kingdom pending) but undated by anyone. Verdict: fully known in direction and roughly in size; the December 2026 start of CE-marked sales (RedEye) is less widely understood and makes a soft Q4 heart figure a likely surprise to retail.
  9. The FDA timeline (brokers late 2027 to early 2028; the TransMedics OCS Heart precedent of nearly three years). Both brokers; RedEye moved its date on 23 October; Jefferies' TransMedics coverage frames the competitive field; a surgical-research director's view that a panel would add six to twelve months is in expert calls. Verdict: known as a date range; the base rates (a 92 percent major-deficiency rate; 320 days with a panel) are not in any broker note, so a deficiency letter would be read as news rather than as the modal outcome.
  10. The liver 510(k) route. Modelled by both brokers (RedEye moved its United States liver launch to 2028 from 2029; DNB Carnegie calls it "clarity on the route"); not discussed by retail. Verdict: known and now in the numbers as upside; the undefined clinical requirement is not.
  11. Hub throughput; the Oklahoma failure. No broker discloses or estimates throughput; the Oklahoma programme's failure appears only in expert calls (Duke); retail debates hubs in general terms. Verdict: unknown to the narrative; the first throughput disclosure (plausibly the Q1 2027 report) would be news either way.
  12. OPO decertification and consolidation. Jefferies (on TransMedics) and DNB Carnegie treat it as a tailwind; expert calls split (a former Xvivo director sees a shake-up; a liver surgeon sees "minimal" direct effect). Verdict: known as a theme, not as a two-quarter contracting pause in H1 2027.
  13. The working-capital release and the cash turn. DNB Carnegie models free cash flow of -97 (2026) and -80 (2027) with intangible capital spending rising to 204; RedEye notes positive quarterly free cash flow in 2026 without a multi-year view; retail does not discuss cash. Verdict: the cash story is not in the narrative at all, and our corrected view (about -8 and +15, then rising) is above both brokers; this is the one dynamic where the surprise would be favourable.
  14. The strategic-buyer floor (OrganOx at about 20 times sales; Paragonix at 11). RedEye cites it in every note as the justification for a premium multiple; DNB Carnegie uses a DCF; Jefferies covers the Terumo transaction. Verdict: fully known; it is the bull narrative's load-bearing argument and its weakness is that it supports the price without supporting the earnings.
  15. The CFO's redeployment with no named successor. RedEye noted it once (15 July 2026); DNB Carnegie frames the strategic role positively; otherwise absent. Verdict: unknown to the narrative; a permanent appointment would be a small relief, a further delay a small negative.

Sell-side versus investor narrative, summarised. The sell-side's framework is "EBITDA margin trajectory plus regulatory catalysts plus a strategic-floor multiple"; it adjusts for timing items, knows the customer risk and the services drag, and is split on amortisation and tax. The investor narrative, as far as the I2 report could observe it on Placera, Avanza and the RedEye community, is "approval dates plus quarterly sales beats"; it celebrated the Q3 sales and net-profit beat without adjusting for 19 of timing and 7 of currency, sold the customer news hard, and holds the 2027 targets as a promise. The TransMedics commentary (Jefferies, Morgan Stanley) treats Xvivo as a credible, cheaper future entrant with superior data and no logistics; it does not model Xvivo and is a read-across for the multiple, not the estimates.

What would surprise the market when the Q4 2026 and FY2027 numbers arrive, in order of size.

  1. The FY2027 EPS level. The narrative's margin framework stops at EBITDA; a 24 percent margin on 1,162 is a 14 percent EBITDA shortfall against consensus but a 35 percent EPS shortfall once 120 of D&A, a 20 percent tax rate and a krona drag are applied. Neither investors nor two of the four brokers have done that arithmetic.
  2. The customer's run-rate once disclosed, if it is nearer -100 than -65.
  3. The dependence of the 2028 consensus on a 2028 United States launch, which the own-3 base rates make the less likely half of the distribution. RedEye's 2028 heart figure above 550 is the single most exposed number in the public record.
  4. The multiple on our earnings: about 40 times 24-month-forward EPS, the 40th percentile of the five-year range, not the 6th. If the market comes to believe our EPS, the "trough valuation" support disappears at the same time as the estimates fall.
  5. Favourably: positive free cash flow in 2027 and a rising net cash balance, against the two brokers' negative numbers, which would blunt any equity-raise anxiety and support the strategic-floor argument with cash rather than with precedents.

The gap, for scoring. The narrative knows our risks in words, so the direction of our view is not a surprise; it does not know them in numbers, so the level will be. The one favourable unknown (cash) is smaller than the unfavourable unknowns (EPS, the customer, the 2028 date, the multiple). The partial offset is positioning: the shares are down 26 percent in six months, short interest is 8 to 9.5 percent and the P/E percentile reading is at its floor, so the price may already discount more than the brokers' numbers do, which would make the EPS cuts, when they come, less damaging than their size implies.

Part 2. Cash flow and balance sheet inputs, one column for each of the five fiscal years

Input FY2026 (year to 31-Dec-26) FY2027 (year to 31-Dec-27) FY2028 (year to 31-Dec-28) FY2029 (year to 31-Dec-29) FY2030 (year to 31-Dec-30)

Working capital is the balance at the end of each fiscal year as a percentage of the last twelve months' revenue.

Note to the Financial Analyst, not an input

The summary's year-end net cash of -24.2 for FY2026 and -18.5 for FY2027 cannot be reconciled with reported net cash of 193 at 30 September 2026 on FactSet's definition (313 of cash; 121 of debt including leases) and free cash flow near zero in Q4; the gap of about 200 sits in the model's opening balance or debt definition and should be checked before the return calculation uses net cash.

Appendix The share-price co-movement table the Investment Analyst was given

Co-movement of XVIVO-SE with its dashboard peers (computed 2026-10-23 from the master dashboard's daily prices)

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.

Peers (from the dashboard data file): Alcon (ALC-CH), Ambu (AMBU.B-DK), BioGaia (BIOG.B-SE), Bonesupport (BONEX-SE), Coloplast (COLO.B-DK), Convatec (CTEC-GB), Demant (DEMANT-DK), Draegerwerk (DRW3-DE), Dynavox (DYVOX-SE), Embla Medical (EMBLA-DK), Fresenius (FRE-DE), Getinge (GETI.B-SE), Medartis (MED-CH), Medacta (MOVE-CH), Ottobock (OBCK-DE), Smith & Nephew (SN-GB), Sonova (SOON-CH), Straumann (STMN-CH), Surgical Science (SUS-SE), Tissue Regenix Group (TRX-GB), Vitrolife (VITR-SE), Ypsomed Holding (YPSN-CH); no price history for AMBU.B-DK, BIOG.B-SE, COLO.B-DK, GETI.B-SE, TRX-GB

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: Xvivo Perfusion AB (XVIVO-SE), a Gothenburg-listed organ-preservation and machine-perfusion company selling proprietary preservation fluids (Perfadex Plus, STEEN Solution), perfusion machines (XPS for lung, Heart Assist Transport, Liver Assist, Kidney Assist Transport) and single-use kits, plus United States organ-recovery, normothermic regional perfusion and FlowHawk workflow-software services; reported in three segments, Thoracic (lung and heart), Abdominal (liver and kidney) and Services.

Request: OWN-1

Question: For Xvivo Perfusion AB (XVIVO-SE), what do the company's own reported balance sheets and cash-flow statements show for net working capital and its components: inventories, trade receivables, trade payables and other current operating items at 31 December 2024, 31 December 2025, 30 June 2026 and 30 September 2026; the working-capital components of operating cash flow for the first nine months of 2026 (the Q3 2026 interim report of 22 October 2026 reports operating cash flow of SEK 181.8 million for the nine months) and for the full years 2024 and 2025; management's commentary on inventory built for the heart launch in 2025 and reduced in 2026, on the Netherlands-to-Sweden manufacturing consolidation's effect on stock, and on expected inventory for the United States heart launch; and how these company figures compare with the net working capital series that data vendors such as FactSet derive for the same dates and with the inventory and receivable days of Ambu, Vitrolife, Bonesupport and TransMedics?

Request: OWN-2

Question: For Xvivo Perfusion AB (XVIVO-SE), what is the composition and outlook of the lines between operating profit and net income: the breakdown of net financial items for each quarter of 2026 and for 2024 and 2025 (exchange-rate differences on cash and receivables, interest income on cash, interest on the EUR 20 million revolving facility and leases, revaluation of contingent consideration or other items), the quarterly tax charges in 2026 and the reasons management gave for them (the Q3 2026 interim report of 22 October 2026 shows a tax credit in the third quarter after charges in the first and second), the deferred tax assets and unrecognised United States tax losses at 30 September 2026, any statement by management or by DNB Carnegie, RedEye, Pareto Securities or Stifel on the expected 2027 and 2028 group tax rate and on the start dates and useful lives of amortisation for the heart PMA and liver 510(k) assets beyond the disclosed SEK 39 million a year for the CE-mark asset, and the precedents at Nordic medtech companies (Bonesupport, Vitrolife, Ambu) for how the effective tax rate moved in the two years after loss carry-forwards were exhausted and United States operations turned profitable?

Request: OWN-3

Question: For Xvivo Perfusion AB (XVIVO-SE), what have Pareto Securities, Stifel, DNB Carnegie, RedEye and any other broker published since the Q3 2026 report of 22 October 2026: their estimates for 2026, 2027 and 2028 revenue, EBITDA, EBIT, net income and earnings per share, their depreciation and amortisation and tax assumptions, their target prices and ratings and the reasons given for each change; what the company's own consensus compilation shows when it is next published ahead of the Q4 2026 report on 28 January 2027; how the share price and short interest have moved since 22 October 2026 and on which news; and what TransMedics' Q3 2026 results on or about 4 November 2026 and Terumo's Organ Technologies quarterly report said about United States heart and liver perfusion volumes, pricing, the National OCS Program and competitive entry, with any comment their covering analysts (Jefferies, Morgan Stanley and others) made about Xvivo's heart entry?

Terms used in the answers

Hover over a dotted term on a computer to see it written out. On a phone, this list is the reference. Terms not listed are company, product or trial names, or terms the Investment Analyst explains where it uses them.

AGM
annual general meeting
AP4
the Fourth Swedish National Pension Fund
CAGR
compound annual growth rate
CAP
continued access protocol: a United States study that lets a device under review keep being used
CE
the European conformity marking a medical device needs to be sold in the European Union
CEO
chief executive officer
CFO
chief financial officer
CMO
chief medical officer
CMS
the Centers for Medicare and Medicaid Services, the United States federal health insurer's agency
COO
chief operating officer
CPT
Current Procedural Terminology: the United States billing codes for medical procedures
D&A
depreciation and amortisation
DBD
donation after brain death
DCD
donation after circulatory death
DCF
discounted cash flow
DNB
DNB Carnegie, a Nordic broker
EBIT
earnings before interest and tax (operating profit)
EBITA
earnings before interest, tax and amortisation
EBITDA
earnings before interest, tax, depreciation and amortisation
ECMO
extracorporeal membrane oxygenation: a machine that oxygenates the blood outside the body
EMEA
Europe, the Middle East and Africa
EPS
earnings per share
EUR
euro
EURIBOR
the euro interbank offered rate
EVLP
ex vivo lung perfusion: assessing and treating donor lungs on a machine outside the body
FDA
the United States Food and Drug Administration
FX
foreign exchange (currency)
FY
fiscal year (FY2027 or FY27 is the company's fiscal year 2027; the company's year-end is in its reports)
H1
first half of the fiscal year
H2
second half of the fiscal year
IFRS
International Financial Reporting Standards
MDR
the European Union's Medical Device Regulation
NHS
the National Health Service (United Kingdom)
NICE
the National Institute for Health and Care Excellence (England)
NRP
normothermic regional perfusion: restoring warm blood flow to the organs inside the donor after circulatory death
OCS
Organ Care System: TransMedics' machine-perfusion platform
OPO
organ procurement organisation: the United States bodies that recover donated organs
OPOs
organ procurement organisations (see OPO)
PGD
primary graft dysfunction: failure of a transplanted organ in the first days
PMA
premarket approval: the Food and Drug Administration's approval route for the highest-risk medical devices
PMAs
premarket approvals (see PMA)
PPE
property, plant and equipment
Q1
first quarter
Q2
second quarter
Q3
third quarter
Q4
fourth quarter
R&D
research and development
SEK
Swedish krona
TSR
total shareholder return
UPMC
University of Pittsburgh Medical Center
USD
United States dollar
XPS
Xvivo Perfusion System: Xvivo's lung-perfusion machine
